## 2026-07-22 13:36 -- Verification failed — escalating to user: agentic-workflow-mqwnc - Doc micro-sweep

**Type:** Work / Verification failure
**Task:** agentic-workflow-mqwnc - Doc micro-sweep — verifier task-file location, stale bc-readme-template pointer, INDEX done-list header wording
**Iteration:** 1 of 3 (iteration hint: task-under-specified — escalated, not re-dispatched)
**Reasons:** the task's acceptance criterion 3 mandates rewording the live `INDEX.md` done-list headers, but INDEX.md is conductor-owned (worker Rule 3) AND the header is machine-generated by `lib/index-rotation.mjs`'s `archivedDoneHeader` — so any worker satisfying the criterion both violates the rule and produces a non-durable edit (regenerated on next rotation). The task spec itself is mis-scoped; a re-dispatch reproduces the violation.
**Salvaged patch:** `.agentheim/salvage/agentic-workflow-mqwnc-escalated-iter1.patch` (holds the two CORRECT non-INDEX doc fixes + the worker's `agentic-workflow-jf6qz` backlog capture)
**Kept worktree:** `.worktrees/agentic-workflow-mqwnc` (branch `aw/agentic-workflow-mqwnc`) — task stays in `doing/` on main
**Next:** builder decides — REFINE via `modeling` (re-scope: land the durable header fix in `lib/index-rotation.mjs` per jf6qz, drop criterion 3's INDEX grep, preserve the two verifier.md/bc-readme-template.md fixes) or abandon

**Summary:** Confirmed the bug is real from the doctrine's own text (BOUNCE integration makes no pre-squash commit; `git merge --squash` can't see the worktree's uncommitted `doing → backlog` move, so `main` never gets it while INDEX/protocol claim it did). Sharpened the fix into a settled design: checkpoint **only the task file** via the `checkpoint` verb (not `git add -A`, which would restage a rebuilt `dashboard/dist/` and defeat ADR-0057) before the squash-merge; a bouncing worker's incidental unverified edits stay out of `main` (salvage patch, ADR-0063, is their channel), so no BOUNCED-return-format change is needed. Added correction of SKILL.md's misleading "other worktree edits the squash picks up" parenthetical. Expanded related_adrs (0037/0032/0057/0063/0026); 4 crisp machine-checkable acceptance criteria.
**Split into:** none
**ADRs written:** none

## 2026-07-13 17:05 -- Modeling / Refined: agentic-workflow-n4qte - Prompt bar greys out the model selector — and warns loudly — when the live bridge is too old to honour it

**Type:** Modeling / Refine
**BC:** agentic-workflow
**Status after:** todo
**Summary:** Re-read the task against the now-landed `infrastructure-v8r3q` and closed two gaps. (1) The `What` only rewired `modelLocked`, leaving `modelLabel`/`modelHint` keyed on `bridgePresent` (board.js L1287-1292) — against a stale bridge that renders a *locked button still reading "Opus"*, re-creating the exact silent lie the task exists to kill; both now key off `bridgeSupportsModel`, and an AC pins the "Default" label. (2) **Builder ruling:** the skew banner now fires on *any* missing capability (`present && KNOWN_CAPABILITIES.some(c => !capabilities.includes(c))`), not on `'model'` specifically — a `'model'`-only trigger would go silent for a future bridge that ships `model` but lacks a fourth field, the very drift class `infrastructure-v8r3q`'s structural guard exists to prevent. Cost, knowingly accepted: the banner copy goes generic ("some launch options") instead of naming model/session-name. This introduces an exported `KNOWN_CAPABILITIES` on the dashboard side (peer of, not import of, the extension's `CAPABILITIES`) and a mirrored dashboard-side structural guard — the extension had one, `bridge-launch.js`'s three hand-written `caps.includes()` gates had none. Also settled the two design questions left open at capture (mount-only probe cadence; `--obligation` token family) and verified every contract the task consumes exists on disk.
**Split into:** none
**ADRs written:** none

## 2026-07-13 16:06 -- Modeling / Refined: infrastructure-v8r3q - The bridge advertises what it can honour — a live /health capability handshake, plus a structural guard that stops a fourth silent drift

**Type:** Modeling / Refine
**BC:** infrastructure
**Status after:** todo
**Summary:** Settled the capture's two open forks, then split the task on the existing transport-vs-meaning seam. (1) The capability signal rides **`GET /health`**, not `bridge.json`: `bridge.js` exports `CAPABILITIES`, and `/health` returns it from the answering process's own in-memory constant, so it structurally cannot go stale — unlike `bridge.json`, which a *separate* process writes last-writer-wins across concurrent VS Code windows. A 0.2.0 bridge omits the field entirely, so **absent ⇒ `LEGACY_CAPABILITIES = ['prompt','skipPermissions']`** — no version arithmetic, no dashboard-side version→feature mapping table to keep in sync. (2) Loudness: grey-out (extending h5wnq's bridge-*absent* lock to bridge-*too-old*) **and** a one-time dismissible banner, since a builder who never opens the model selector would otherwise never learn his extension is stale — and the skew silently affects every launch's session name too, not just the one control. (3) The capture's "same for the session-name field" AC was rewritten rather than kept: there *is* no name control (`nameForPromptMode` always derives it from the mode), so the banner covers it; no phantom affordance invented. Refinement also surfaced a hole the capture missed — a render-time UI gate cannot stop a fire-time bug — so `runOnBridge` now omits `model`/`name` at the **wire level** whenever the live probe says the listener can't honour them: defense in depth, not merely UI courtesy. The structural guard is executable, not a fourth prose rule: a test scans `makeHandler`'s `parsed?.<field>` reads and asserts that set equals `CAPABILITIES` in both directions, so adding a field without declaring it breaks the build.
**Split into:** agentic-workflow-n4qte (prompt bar grey-out + skew banner + DOM-harness regression coverage; `depends_on: [infrastructure-v8r3q]`)
**ADRs written:** ADR-0018 (amended in place — its 4th amendment; records the `/health`-is-authoritative rule, the absent-⇒-baseline default, the two-place defense in depth, and the structural guard)

## 2026-07-13 16:05 -- Modeling / Captured: design-system-me97j - ModelSplitButton's `disabled` deadens the model caret along with Enter

**Type:** Modeling / Capture
**BC:** design-system
**Filed to:** todo
**Summary:** The builder reported that with an empty prompt the greyed-out Enter button also deadens the model combo beside it — but picking a model before typing is exactly when you'd want to. Diagnosis: `ModelSplitButton` has one interactivity gate for two regions — `design-system-r9dtm` carried `EnterButton`'s single-region `disabled` prop (`design-system-tfhn6`) across the whole widget, so `canOpenMenu = !locked && !disabled`, the caret gets the real `disabled` attribute (leaving the tab order too), and the 0.55 opacity sits on the wrapper enclosing both. The consumer exposes it: `board.js` passes `disabled=${!canFire}` where `canFire` is derived purely from prompt-blankness — no in-flight state — so `disabled` only ever means "nothing to launch", which says nothing about the caret. Fix: narrow `disabled` to the primary region; the caret's sole gate becomes `locked` (which removes it entirely anyway). Builder decided at capture to dim **only the Enter half** rather than keep the whole surface dimmed — a control painted dead that still responds to clicks is a lying affordance. No `board.js` change needed; no `dist/` rebuild (ADR-0057, conductor's job). Filed straight to **todo**: cause confirmed in source, the visual decision is made, and the existing DOM harness makes every criterion mutation-provable.

## 2026-07-13 14:36 -- Modeling / Captured: infrastructure-v8r3q - The bridge contract grows but never versions — an old bridge silently swallows fields the dashboard sends

**Type:** Modeling / Capture
**BC:** infrastructure
**Filed to:** backlog
**Summary:** The builder reported that the dashboard's model selector and session-rename had no effect — choosing Sonnet launched an Opus session, and named sessions still read "Claude". Investigation found no code defect: bridge 0.4.0 (carrying both features) was installed at 14:09, but both live VS Code extension hosts had started at 09:08/09:36 and were still executing 0.2.0 from memory, which honours only `prompt` + `skipPermissions` and hard-codes `createTerminal({name:'Claude'})`. A window reload fixes the instance. The captured defect is the class beneath it: `BRIDGE_V` has stayed at `1` through three growths of the `/run` contract (`skipPermissions` infrastructure-016, `name` c6fzb, `model` h5wnq), so an old bridge silently drops unknown fields, returns `202 {ok:true}`, and the dashboard reports success — version skew degrades indistinguishably from working.

## 2026-07-13 14:34 -- Modeling / Captured: design-system-k3f7q - ModelSplitButton's model menu opens upward and escapes the prompt console's clip

**Type:** Modeling / Capture
**BC:** design-system
**Filed to:** todo
**Summary:** The prompt console's model-select menu is unreadable — sheared off by the prompt segment. Diagnosis found **two stacked causes, neither individually sufficient**: `ModelSplitButton`'s panel is hard-anchored `top: calc(100% + 6px)` (so it opens *downward*, into the bottom edge of the viewport, since the console is bottom-docked per aw-bz3az), AND the console `<section>` carries an `overflow: hidden` (`board.js` ~1285) that clips every popover inside it — a clip that exists only to round the mode-tab row's end cells. The fix is one indivisible change across the ds/aw seam: anchor the menu on `bottom:` **unconditionally** (builder-decided at capture — no `menuPlacement` prop, no collision auto-flip; the split button's only home is the bottom-docked console, and Quick Capture pins the model via `locked`), and move the clip off the section onto the tab row that actually needs it. Captured as **ONE design-system task** rather than the usual ds→aw pair (the ds-009/ds-014/ds-020 ordering) because a split would ship two individually invisible, unverifiable halves — the `design-system-015` precedent already has a design-system task editing `dashboard/app/board.js` and rebuilding `dist/` when the change is indivisible. Filed straight to **todo**: diagnosis confirmed in source, placement decided, and the `infrastructure-d2n8s` DOM harness makes both halves mutation-provable rather than source-regex-asserted.

## 2026-07-13 14:05 -- Work session ended

**Type:** Work / Session end
**Duration:** 42m (batch start 13:23 → 14:05)
**Completed:** 2 (first-try PASS: 2, re-dispatched: 0, skipped: 0)
**Bounced:** 0
**Failed:** 0
**Escalated after verification:** 0
**Dispatches:** infrastructure-d2n8s: 1, agentic-workflow-q7v3k: 1
**Commits:** 5 (2 batch-start + 2 task completions + this session-end entry)
**Vision-conformance:** none — batch aligns with vision. Both shipped tasks pull directly *toward* **"Wrong work is caught by structure, not luck"** — they are that criterion, made load-bearing: `d2n8s` replaces source-regex tests (structurally blind to live behavior) with a DOM harness that can actually observe the bug class that shipped `m2vkp`, and `q7v3k` replaces a prompt-level plea with a mechanical filter at the one seam a derived artifact can escape through. `q7v3k` also serves **"Independent work runs in parallel … without two workers colliding on the same file"** — the bundle was precisely the file two parallel workers could silently collide on. No non-goal is approached. Non-goal 3 (*Not autonomous*) held: both verifier gates ran and the human remains the decider. Non-goal 5 (*Not a SaaS*) untouched — all state stays in `.agentheim/`. Non-goal 4 (*Not stack-prescriptive*) deserves an explicit note since this session **added a dependency** (jsdom): it does not bite. jsdom is a devDependency of *Agentheim's own dashboard*, prescribing nothing about a user's project stack — the same category ADR-0003 already blessed for esbuild. Zero flags; **no whats-next advisory written** — a clean batch must not clobber a genuine recommendation with an all-clear.
**Carry-over:** `inspiration/`: left behind (owner: builder reference material — one file, "Agentheim UX Explorations.html"; an untracked UX-explorations folder, not project bookkeeping, never `work`'s to commit; same disposition as the 2026-07-09 15:29/16:03/16:44/18:02/19:26 and 2026-07-13 10:45/12:30 sessions). No stranded worktrees — both (`d2n8s`, `q7v3k`) torn down at integration, each with its `dashboard/node_modules` junction unlinked *before* `git worktree remove` (the mandatory ordering; the shared `node_modules` confirmed intact at 41 entries after each teardown).
**Notes:** Two tasks, two waves, both first-try PASS. Suite green end-to-end at **1336 pass / 2 fail** — the 2 being the known `vscode-extension/test/bridge.test.mjs` EADDRINUSE-on-31425 pair (the builder's live VS Code bridge), pre-existing and briefed to every worker and verifier up front so nobody burned an iteration "fixing" them. Net +22 tests (1315 baseline → 9 added / 3 retired by `d2n8s`, +13 by `q7v3k`). Wave 2 was not planned: `q7v3k` was promoted to `todo` by a concurrent `modeling` session *while* `d2n8s` was in flight, and the Phase-2 re-scan picked it up — the mid-run pickup working exactly as designed. Three things worth keeping.

**(1) The session's headline finding: workers were NEVER defying the `dist/` contract. The test suite was rebuilding it for them.** `q7v3k`'s entire `## Why` rested on the premise that workers rebuilt `dashboard/dist/` across four sessions in defiance of an explicit prohibition — *"a rule that is violated by 100% of agents who are explicitly told the rule is not a rule; it is a wish."* That diagnosis is **false**, and the conductor caught it before dispatch by reading the file the task never checked: `dashboard/test/dist-build.test.mjs` has a `before()` hook that runs `node build.mjs` on **every single suite invocation**. Since TDD *requires* a worker to run the suite, **every worker rebuilds `dist/` mechanically, without ever typing the command.** Compliance was never possible — a worker could only have obeyed by refusing to run the tests. This was handed to the worker as pre-dispatch intelligence, independently re-verified by the verifier, and the task's own `## Why` was corrected in place before it landed in `done/`. It does not weaken the chosen guard; it **decisively strengthens** it: if the rebuild is mechanically unavoidable, then no prompt can ever prevent it, and filtering the conductor's staging seam — making the artifact *inert* rather than *forbidden* — is the only enforcement that can work at all. It also answered the task's own open thread (*"did some workers escape the worktree?"*) in the negative: nobody escaped, the rebuild is in-worktree and test-induced, and the sibling task the Notes contemplated is **not needed**. Standing lesson: **a task's stated rationale is not evidence.** This one had survived a capture and a refinement with the falsifying file one `grep` away.

**(2) The verifier gate is now demonstrably worth its spend — and it earned it by MUTATING, not reading.** Both verifiers were told to *prove the tests could go red* rather than observe them pass, and both performed real mutations instead of reasoning about them. `d2n8s`'s verifier removed `shouldWindowCtrlMHandle` from `board.js` and got the exact m2vkp signature back (`'Haiku' !== 'Sonnet'` — the +2 parity trap), then went **beyond its brief**: it flipped `arrowDirection` in *styleguide* source and watched two *dashboard* tests turn red, which independently proves the new harness mounts **real cross-BC source**, not a copy — a property no one had asked it to check. `q7v3k`'s verifier ran the mutation that actually mattered — reverting the guard to a naive relative-string match, the "looks correct, passes its own test, inert against every real input" trap the task named as its highest-priority hazard — and **6 tests went red.** The guard genuinely catches it. After two sessions in which workers shipped tests structurally incapable of failing, mutation-under-verification has now caught the *absence* of that failure mode twice. This should be standing verifier doctrine, not a per-dispatch reminder.

**(3) The `dist/` phantom and the guard met in the same session, by accident.** While integrating `q7v3k` the conductor watched the exact phenomenon the task describes: running the suite in the worktree left `dashboard/dist/{app.js,index.html}` dirty with **zero line changes** (`git diff --numstat` empty — the known `autocrlf` phantom), and the worker had correctly kept them out of its `FILE_LIST`. That is precisely the input `partitionCheckpointFiles` now filters, and precisely why the guard was specified to operate on **declared data rather than the working tree** — a `git status`-based guard would have been fooled by the phantom in both directions. Separately, on `d2n8s`'s integration, regenerating `dist/` from merged source produced a **byte-identical** bundle (the one-word `export` added to `BoardPromptBar` is invisible to the bundle), and `grep -c jsdom dashboard/dist/app.js` returned **0** — the "jsdom never reaches `dist/`" criterion verified empirically, not asserted.

No new backlog items were created by workers, and no concept candidates were reported. ADR-0056 (jsdom resolve hook, `scope: infrastructure`) and ADR-0057 (derived-artifact checkpoint guard, `scope: agentic-workflow`) were written; ADR-0032's `### Windows & node_modules` section was amended so it no longer licenses the very behavior ADR-0057 guards against. One numbering collision was caught at dispatch: `q7v3k` had been refined to claim "ADR-0056", which `d2n8s` took ~20 minutes earlier while `q7v3k` sat in `todo` — a real hazard of refining tasks ahead of a session that mints ADRs, and one the conductor can only catch by re-checking the next free number at dispatch rather than trusting the task file.

## 2026-07-13 13:52 -- Modeling / Refined: agentic-workflow-q7v3k - Make the "workers never rebuild dist/" contract structural

**Type:** Modeling / Refine
**BC:** agentic-workflow
**Status after:** todo (auto-promoted — cleared the readiness gate during refinement)
**Summary:** The capture deliberately left ONE open question — *where does the guard fire* — with three candidates (suite test / conductor-side check / git-level guard) and an instruction that a worker pick one with an argument. Refinement closed it, and in doing so found that **one of the three candidates was not merely weak but inverted**. **Decision: filter the conductor's checkpoint `git add`, mechanized.** The insight: a rebuilt `dist/` has exactly ONE channel out of a worktree — the conductor's enumerated stage at `skills/work/SKILL.md` ~line 111, which today stages the worker's self-reported `FILE_LIST` **verbatim, on trust, unfiltered**. A worker may dirty its worktree freely; if the derived artifact is never *staged*, it never reaches the squash-merge and the violation is inert. So the fix replaces judgment with transcription at a seam that already exists — the same move ADR-0038 made for the lifecycle verbs — via a pure `lib/derived-artifact-guard.mjs` (ADR-0052 family) consumed by a new `checkpoint` verb on the lifecycle CLI. **This answers the task's stated crux — sanctioned vs. forbidden writer — with NO actor check anywhere:** the guard lives in a verb that only ever runs against a worktree, so the conductor's rebuild-from-merged-source on `main` is not *exempted*, it is **out of reach by construction**. **(a) is rejected as actively harmful, not just insufficient:** the verifier runs the suite *from the worktree*, so "dist matches a fresh build of current source" is true exactly when the worker committed the violation and false when a worker correctly left dist alone while changing source that feeds it — that test would have **coerced every dashboard-touching worker into rebuilding**. It is now an explicit anti-criterion so nobody re-proposes it. **(c) is rejected as the wrong actor on settled ground:** workers never run git (ADR-0026/0032), so a hook fires on the conductor and never on the violator; and ADR-0013 already decided against CI/hooks for this repo (no `core.hooksPath`, no hooks, no `.gitattributes` — verified). **Two findings the capture could not have known, both of which make the rule weaker than it looked.** First, **the contract has no durable home**: `rebuild` and `build.mjs` appear in **zero** files under `skills/` and `agents/` — the "HARD CONTRACT" existed only in prose the conductor improvised into each dispatch, re-typed every session. Workers weren't defying a written rule; they were defying a rule that was never written down. Second, **ADR-0032 currently licenses the behaviour**: its `### Windows & node_modules` section says esbuild "writes each worktree's own tracked `dashboard/dist/`" — so a worker reading the architecture is *told* building in its worktree is expected. Amending that passage is now an AC; without it the guard enforces a rule the architecture contradicts. Highest-priority implementation trap recorded: `FILE_LIST` is **absolute, OS-native-separator** paths (`references/worker-return-format.md` line 17) — a guard or test written against a POSIX-relative literal like `'dashboard/dist/app.js'` will pass its own test and be **structurally inert on every real input**, the exact class of never-red artifact this session already shipped twice. Also carried: an open thread (the historical rebuilds may mean workers escaped their worktree and dirtied `main` directly — a distinct defect this guard only half-covers; check before implementing) and a deliberate spin-out (`.gitattributes` `dashboard/dist/** -text -merge`, an infrastructure follow-up). Routing re-confirmed as `agentic-workflow` on the capture's own test: `dashboard/build.mjs` is untouched; every deliverable lands in the work loop.
**Split into:** none
**ADRs written:** none yet — ADR-0056 is specified and drafted for the worker to write during execution (`scope: agentic-workflow`, ADR-0026/0032/0038/0055 lineage), plus an amendment to ADR-0032.

## 2026-07-13 13:20 -- Modeling / Refined: infrastructure-d2n8s - A DOM-render test harness

**Type:** Modeling / Refine
**BC:** infrastructure
**Status after:** todo (auto-promoted — cleared the readiness gate during refinement)
**Summary:** Closed all three of the capture's open scope questions and attached an architect's pre-flight pass that found one hard blocker and one false-green trap the capture could not have known about. **(1) Library: jsdom, decided — not a spike.** It's the reference DOM impl and React 18 `createRoot` attaches delegated listeners to the *root container*, so the m2vkp mechanism is plain spec capture/bubble behavior, not browser-specific. The "must reproduce m2vkp / removing the guard turns it RED" criterion survives as a hard gate, and a jsdom that fails it is a **bounce**, not an invitation to substitute happy-dom. **(2) Home: `dashboard/package.json`, and it reaches the styleguide from there.** Structural fact: `dashboard/` is the only tree in the repo with a dependency graph and a real `node_modules/` — and `react`/`react-dom`/`htm` are **already devDependencies there**, which also kills the ADR-0002/0003 "no install step" objection on the facts. The styleguide has *no package.json at all*, so it cannot host the harness; but `build.mjs` already reaches across the BC boundary into `styleguide/app/`, so a `dashboard/test/` file can mount a styleguide component by relative import. Confirms the routing to `infrastructure`. **(3) Scope: harness + the Ctrl+M collapse + `ModelSplitButton`'s keyboard contract** — not a big-bang regex migration, but not harness-plus-one-test either: converting the styleguide component is what *proves the cross-BC reach works* rather than deferring that discovery to the next a11y task. **Two findings materially changed the ACs.** First, a **hard blocker the capture missed**: `board.js` and the styleguide modules it pulls in import `react`/`htm` as **bare specifiers**, and Node ESM resolves those by walking up from the *importing file* — from a styleguide module that walk finds no `node_modules` anywhere, so `import('../app/board.js')` in a test throws `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. esbuild only works today because `build.mjs` hands it `nodePaths`; **Node has no `nodePaths` and ignores `NODE_PATH` for ESM.** The task now leads with a `module.register()` resolve hook as AC #1 (~30 lines, zero source changes) and explicitly **forbids** the tempting alternative — junctioning `styleguide/node_modules` → `dashboard/node_modules` — because ADR-0032's spike found `git worktree remove --force` recurses through an un-unlinked junction and *silently deletes the real target's contents*. That resolver decision is flagged as ADR-worthy. Second, a **false-green trap**: on a freshly-mounted `BoardPromptBar`, `modelLocked = !bridgePresent || isModelLockedForMode(mode)` — and on mount `bridgePresent` is false **and** the mode is Quick Capture, so **Ctrl+M is a no-op on both counts**. A naive mount-and-dispatch test would pass *trivially, because nothing happens*, and would survive the mutation check — the exact "structurally incapable of failing" artifact this session already produced twice (12:30 entry, observation 2). Getting the bug *live* is now its own AC: stub `fetch` for **both** bridge calls (`discoverBridge` **and** `probeHealth` — Marco's real bridge may be listening, so a half-stub makes the test depend on whether VS Code is open) and move off Quick Capture first. Notes also carry the worktree bootstrap hazard (a bare `npm install` inside a worktree writes **through** the junction into the main tree's shared `node_modules`, violating ADR-0032's read-only premise — safe sequence given), the jsdom `navigator` getter-only trap, the **attach-the-container-or-the-event-never-reaches-`document`** trap (the easiest way to ship a worthless harness), `act()` discipline, and mount-teardown leakage.
**Split into:** none
**ADRs written:** none (the resolver-hook decision is flagged for the worker to write during execution, once proven empirically rather than on paper)

## 2026-07-13 12:45 -- Modeling / Captured: agentic-workflow-q7v3k, infrastructure-d2n8s - Two guards the work session earned

**Type:** Modeling / Capture
**BC:** agentic-workflow + infrastructure (two seams, one origin)
**Filed to:** backlog (both — builder wants to review before they're worked)
**Summary:** Both items fell out of the 2026-07-13 12:30 work session (observations 2 and 4 of its session-end entry) and are captured with that evidence attached rather than as hunches. **`agentic-workflow-q7v3k`** makes the "workers never rebuild `dashboard/dist/`" contract **structural**: prompt-level prohibition has now failed across FOUR sessions — in the last one all three workers rebuilt it on their first pass, including one given an explicit "HARD CONTRACT, not a preference" framing. This is a correctness problem, not tidiness: esbuild tree-shakes unused exports (`probeBridge` was absent from one worker's bundle purely because nothing consumed it yet), so a worker's bundle and a post-merge bundle legitimately differ, and a textual 3-way merge of two workers' bundles yields an artifact built from a source tree that never existed — plausible-looking, silently missing a shipped fix, invisible to a suite that imports `app/` not `dist/`. The enforcement point (suite test vs. conductor-side check vs. git-level guard) is deliberately left open for refinement, since each has a real flaw and the guard must not flag the conductor's own *sanctioned* regeneration. Prior art: `lib/agent-spawn-namespace.mjs` (ADR-0052) and `agentic-workflow-080` are the existing live-tree-lint family. **`infrastructure-d2n8s`** stands up a DOM-render test harness (jsdom/happy-dom) as a test-time devDependency, because the board and styleguide suites are **source-regex** suites — they read code as text — and that is structurally blind to live event-propagation behavior. It shipped a real bug: `m2vkp`'s double-handled Ctrl+M (React 18 `createRoot` delegates keydown at the root container, the event bubbles on to a `document` listener, neither called `stopPropagation()`) cycled the model by +2, making Fable and Sonnet unreachable while typing — with 1279 tests green. The ADR-0002/0003 "no install step" objection was checked at capture and **dissolves**: those ADRs constrain *running* the dashboard, and ADR-0003 already blesses esbuild as a build-time-only dependency; a dev-only test library is the same category. Its load-bearing acceptance criterion is that the harness must be able to **reproduce the m2vkp bug** — a harness that cannot faithfully model React 18's event delegation has failed its one job and should be bounced, not shipped as false confidence.

## 2026-07-13 12:30 -- Work session ended

**Type:** Work / Session end
**Duration:** 1h09m (batch start 11:21 → 12:30)
**Completed:** 3 (first-try PASS: 1, re-dispatched: 2, skipped: 0)
**Bounced:** 0
**Failed:** 0
**Escalated after verification:** 0
**Dispatches:** infrastructure-h5wnq: 1, design-system-r9dtm: 2, agentic-workflow-m2vkp: 2
**Commits:** 6 (2 batch-start + 3 task completions + this session-end entry)
**Vision-conformance:** none — batch aligns with vision. All three shipped tasks are one idea (a session-model selector on the prompt bar) split across three seams, and none pulls toward a stated non-goal. The batch's strongest pull is *toward* **"Wrong work is caught by structure, not luck"**: the verifier gate rejected plausible-but-wrong output **twice**, and in both cases the defect was invisible to a green test suite (see Notes). Non-goal 3 (*"Not autonomous"*) is untouched — every gate held and the human remains the one who decides what the models are for. Non-goal 5 (*"Not a SaaS"*) is likewise untouched: persistence is deliberately **in-page React state only** (ADR-0017), no `localStorage`, no server write, no file. Non-goal 4 (*"Not stack-prescriptive"*) does not bite — `PROMPT_MODELS` names Claude models because Agentheim *is* a Claude Code plugin; it prescribes nothing about the user's project stack. Zero flags; **no whats-next advisory written** — a clean batch must not clobber a genuine recommendation with an all-clear.
**Carry-over:** `inspiration/`: left behind (owner: builder reference material — the untracked UX-explorations folder; not project bookkeeping, never `work`'s to commit; same disposition as the 2026-07-09 15:29/16:03/16:44/18:02/19:26 and 2026-07-13 10:45 sessions). No stranded worktrees — all three (`h5wnq`, `r9dtm`, `m2vkp`) torn down at integration, each with its `dashboard/node_modules` junction unlinked *before* `git worktree remove` (the mandatory ordering; the shared `node_modules` was confirmed intact after every teardown).
**Notes:** Three workers, two waves, all three BCs, suite green end-to-end at **1287 pass / 0 fail** (1214 pre-session baseline + 17 + 23 + 33). The DAG did real work: `m2vkp` `depends_on` BOTH wave-1 tasks and genuinely could not start until they landed, so wave 2 was a dependency ordering, not a `MAX_PARALLEL` throttle. Five observations worth keeping.

**(1) The verifier gate earned the whole session, twice — and both defects were invisible to a green suite.** `m2vkp` iteration 1 shipped a **double-handled `Ctrl+M`**: `onPromptKeyDown`'s `CYCLE_MODEL` branch and the window-scoped `document` keydown listener BOTH called `setSelectedModel`, and under React 18 `createRoot` the delegated keydown fires at the root container and then *continues bubbling to `document`* — neither handler called `stopPropagation()`, and `preventDefault()` does not stop propagation. Net step `+2`. With exactly four models that is a **parity trap**: from the Opus default it cycled Opus → Haiku → Opus → Haiku, leaving **Fable and Sonnet unreachable whenever the prompt field had focus** — i.e. in the *normal* case, while typing a prompt. No error, no crash, 1279 tests green. This is the archetypal plausible-but-wrong output the vision names as the keystone problem, and only a fresh-context adversarial read of the *wiring* caught it.

**(2) Twice this session a worker wrote a test that was STRUCTURALLY INCAPABLE of failing on the criterion it was named for.** `design-system-r9dtm`'s guard `test('no Agentheim-specific model names appear anywhere in the styleguide')` scanned `button.js`/`app.js` but **not** `button-state.js` — the one file it had just leaked into — and its regex omitted `Fable` entirely. `m2vkp`'s `Ctrl+M` test asserted that *both* handlers contain `setSelectedModel(` — i.e. it **asserted the buggy shape** and passed *because* the bug was present. A test that cannot go red on its own criterion is worse than no test: it manufactures false confidence and it is the exact artifact a fail-closed verifier is supposed to distrust. **Both were caught only because the verifier was told to prove the test could fail, not merely observe it pass.** That instruction should become standing verifier doctrine rather than a per-dispatch reminder — it is now 2-for-2.

**(3) The task's own "open question" was wrong, and answering it at dispatch cost one `claude --help` call.** Both `h5wnq` and `m2vkp` carried a Note speculating *"Fable may not have a short alias — verify, don't guess."* The CLI's own help documents `--model` accepting *"an alias for the latest model (e.g. 'fable', 'opus', or 'sonnet')"*. Fable **does** have one. The conductor resolved this once, before dispatch, and handed the evidence to both workers — so the bridge's `MODEL_ALLOWLIST` and the board's `PROMPT_MODELS` agree by construction (`fable`/`opus`/`sonnet`/`haiku`) rather than by two workers independently guessing. Worth noting *why* this mattered: a full id like `claude-opus-4-8` is **not** in the allowlist and would have degraded silently to *no `--model` flag* — a working launch on the wrong model, with no error. Resolving shared unknowns at the conductor seam, once, is cheaper and safer than N workers each rediscovering them.

**(4) Workers rebuild `dashboard/dist/` off-contract, and prompt-level prohibition does not hold it.** All three workers were told not to; **all three did it anyway** on their first pass (`m2vkp` even after an explicit "HARD CONTRACT, not a preference" framing — though it complied on iteration 1 and only relapsed on iteration 2, plausibly as a side-effect of booting the dashboard to test its fix). Every rebuild was reverted and the bundle regenerated from merged source at integration, per doctrine — and that doctrine is load-bearing: `probeBridge` is **tree-shaken out of the bundle** because nothing consumed it until `m2vkp` landed, so a worker's bundle and a post-merge bundle legitimately differ, and a textual merge of two workers' bundles would produce an artifact built from a source tree that never existed. This is now **four sessions running**. It is no longer a fluke; it wants a structural guard (a lint asserting `dist/` is unchanged in a worker diff), not another sentence in the dispatch template.

**(5) The conductor's own near-miss, recorded honestly.** While investigating the `dist/` churn the conductor ran `git stash` mid-integration to test build reproducibility, and the `stash pop` then collided with its own rebuild — leaving the squash-merge staged state briefly in doubt. It was recovered cleanly (`git checkout -- dashboard/dist/app.js` then `git stash pop`), and no work was lost. Lesson: **do not run `git stash` while a squash-merge is staged.** Diagnostic curiosity is not worth risking the one tree only the conductor may write. Investigate on a throwaway worktree instead.

No new backlog items were created by workers, and no concept candidates were reported. Two improvements surfaced for the builder to file: a structural `dist/` guard (see 4), and a DOM-render test harness — the verifier noted that `m2vkp`'s "advances by exactly one" proof and its "board.js actually calls the guard" proof are necessarily **split across two files** (a behavioral test against the pure exports + a source-regex test pinning the call site) because the project has no DOM harness; together they close the loop, but one true end-to-end assertion would be strictly better.

## 2026-07-13 11:20 -- Modeling / Captured: agentic-workflow-m2vkp, infrastructure-h5wnq, design-system-r9dtm - Prompt-bar model selector

**Type:** Modeling / Capture
**BC:** agentic-workflow + infrastructure + design-system (one idea, three seams)
**Filed to:** todo (all three)
**Summary:** The prompt bar's second row carries two launch affordances — a bordered `↵` hint glyph and the ochre `EnterButton`. The builder wants one: the ochre button, widened, naming the model the session it launches will run on, with a caret that opens a model menu (Fable / Opus / Sonnet / Haiku). Quick Capture is pinned to Haiku; every other mode is selectable, defaulting to Opus. Ctrl+M cycles. Both the selected mode tab and the selected model now survive a launch — which REVERSES ADR-0050's original post-launch reset-to-Quick-Capture rule (`setHighlightedMode(DEFAULT_PROMPT_MODE_INDEX)` in `onResult`). Split three ways along the existing seams: `design-system-r9dtm` builds the `ModelSplitButton` primitive (ADR-0003 forbids the board forking it); `infrastructure-h5wnq` threads a `model` field through `POST /run` → `claude --model <id>` argv and exports the ambient `probeBridge()` the grey-out needs (bridge presence is only probed lazily at fire time today); `agentic-workflow-m2vkp` consumes both, adds the pure `prompt-model.js` module and a fifth disjoint `CYCLE_MODEL` key intent, and writes ADR-0050's fifth amendment. Builder rulings taken at capture: in-page persistence only (no localStorage — ADR-0017 untouched), Opus as the non-Quick-Capture default, and the selector greys out with no bridge (a clipboard-copied command cannot carry `--model`, so the control must not promise one). Two notes carried into the tasks so no worker has to rediscover them: the Fable CLI alias is unverified (check `claude --help`, don't guess), and this is a SESSION-model selector that composes with — never overrides — ADR-0031's per-agent model pins.

## 2026-07-13 10:45 -- Work session ended

**Type:** Work / Session end
**Duration:** 50m (batch start 09:55 → 10:45)
**Completed:** 4 (first-try PASS: 4, re-dispatched: 0, skipped: 0)
**Bounced:** 0
**Failed:** 0
**Escalated after verification:** 0
**Dispatches:** agentic-workflow-rwxms: 1, agentic-workflow-tkq7v: 1, agentic-workflow-spv0k: 1, infrastructure-c6fzb: 1
**Commits:** 7 (2 batch-start + 4 task completions + this session-end entry)
**Vision-conformance:** none — batch aligns with vision. All four shipped tasks pull *toward* stated success criteria and away from none. `rwxms` serves *"Wrong work is caught by structure, not luck"* most directly: it makes the invariant-violating half-applied claim (source rewritten to the destination's status, still sitting in the origin folder) **structurally unreachable** rather than merely unlikely — the mover now writes the destination and only then unlinks the source, so every residual failure downgrades from an undetectable status-matches-folder violation to a detectable, self-healing duplicate. `spv0k` serves the same criterion one layer up, at the human interface: the launch feedback had been *lying* — reporting "Quick Capture launched" when the builder fired Modeling — and a feedback channel the builder cannot trust is precisely the "plausible-looking and quietly wrong" failure the vision names as the keystone problem. `tkq7v` (returning Ctrl+←/→ to native word-jump, moving mode-cycling to Tab/Shift+Tab, with Escape as the WCAG keyboard exit) and `c6fzb` (bridge-launched sessions carry a derived name via `claude -n`) are ergonomics and infrastructure with no vision tension — c6fzb keeps all state local to the repo, so non-goal 5 (*"Not a SaaS / not multi-tenant"*) is untouched, and nothing in the batch touches non-goals 1, 2, 3 or 4. Zero flags; **no whats-next advisory written** — a clean batch must not clobber a genuine recommendation with an all-clear.
**Carry-over:** `inspiration/`: left behind (owner: builder reference material — the untracked UX-explorations folder cited by `design-system-v08qq` as its source; not project bookkeeping, never `work`'s to commit; same disposition as the 2026-07-09 15:29, 16:03, 16:44, 18:02 and 19:26 sessions). No stranded worktrees — all four (`rwxms`, `tkq7v`, `spv0k`, `c6fzb`) torn down at integration, each with its `dashboard/node_modules` junction unlinked *before* `git worktree remove` (the mandatory ordering; the shared `node_modules` was confirmed intact after every teardown). A transient `.worktrees/_baseline` detached probe, created to confirm the two bridge test failures pre-date this session, was removed the same way.
**Notes:** Four workers, two waves, both BCs, **all four first-try verified PASS** — no re-dispatch, no bounce, no failure, no escalation. Six observations worth keeping.

**(1) The first REAL merge-back conflict since ADR-0032 landed, and the doctrine held.** `tkq7v` and `spv0k` both edited `dashboard/app/board.js`, `dashboard/test/board-prompt-bar.test.mjs` and ADR-0050 from the same base commit. The **code auto-merged with zero conflicts** — tkq7v owned `onPromptKeyDown`, spv0k owned `fire()`/`PromptModeTab`, and git's 3-way merge reconciled them cleanly, exactly as ADR-0032 predicted. What conflicted was **prose and a build artifact**, not logic: both workers appended a *fourth* "## Amendment — 2026-07-13" section to ADR-0050 at the same anchor, both rewrote the same BC-README bullet, and both had rebuilt `dashboard/dist/app.js`. Per doctrine the conductor **aborted with `git reset --hard HEAD`** (never `git merge --abort` — a squash merge sets no `MERGE_HEAD`), preserved the losing worktree, and **surfaced to the builder rather than auto-guessing**. The builder chose the resolution. This is the second live confirmation that demoting the conflict pre-scan to advisory is safe — the collision was detected by git at merge-back, not guessed from English at dispatch.

**(2) The bundle conflict was the genuinely dangerous one, and a textual merge would have shipped a lie.** `spv0k`'s branch predated `tkq7v`, so **its `dist/app.js` contained no Tab-cycling code**. Any textual 3-way merge of that minified bundle would have produced an artifact built from a source tree **that never existed** — plausibly clean-looking, silently missing a shipped fix, and invisible to the test suite (which imports `app/`, not `dist/`). The correct resolution was to **discard both sides and rebuild from the merged source**, then assert *both* fixes are present in the output (`Tab"&&` branch → 1 hit; `flashed` prop → 2 hits). **Generated artifacts must be regenerated at merge-back, never merged.** This is a general lesson, not a one-off: it will recur on every future parallel batch where two workers touch `dashboard/app/` — worth folding into the skill.

**(3) `rwxms` validated itself in production within minutes of landing.** When claiming the second wave's `infrastructure-c6fzb`, the conductor **deliberately removed the empty `infrastructure/doing/` directory first** so the claim ran against a genuinely absent destination folder — the exact condition that used to throw `ENOENT` *after* rewriting the source to `status: doing`, leaving a half-applied claim requiring a manual `mkdir -p` workaround (the documented field failure, reproduced twice in the WisdomHeim vault). It **backfilled the folder and succeeded cleanly**. The bug closed on its own instigator, in the real lifecycle, on the first opportunity. The long-standing "pre-create `doing/` before claiming" workaround is now **obsolete** and should be dropped from operator memory.

**(4) The `TESTS_ADDED` inflation streak stayed broken — three sessions running, now across four independent recounts.** Every dispatch prompt told the worker plainly that an AC-mandated *rewrite* is not an addition; every verifier prompt told it to recount independently. Results: rwxms claimed 6 → verifier counted 6; tkq7v claimed 3 → 3; spv0k claimed 5 → 5; c6fzb claimed **17** → verifier counted 8+4+4+1 = 17, explicitly **excluding 7 rewrites** it identified in `bridge.test.mjs`. Suite arithmetic corroborates end-to-end: 1013 baseline → 1027 after wave 1 (+6+3+5) → 1059 after wave 2 (+17). This two-sentence prompt fix is now confirmed across seven counts and **should be folded into `references/worker-return-format.md`** rather than re-typed per dispatch — it has earned permanence.

**(5) Two pre-existing test failures are environmental, not a regression — and a verifier would have failed the task without being told.** `vscode-extension/test/bridge.test.mjs`'s two fixed-port tests fail with `EADDRINUSE` on `127.0.0.1:31425` because **the builder's own bridge extension is running in VS Code and holding that port**. Confirmed pre-existing by checking out the pre-session commit `ddabe61` in a throwaway worktree and reproducing them there. Both the worker and the verifier were briefed explicitly ("expect exactly these 2, a third is yours") — without that, a fail-closed verifier would correctly have FAILed a correct task. **The tests bind a fixed port with no environment override, so they are un-runnable whenever the real bridge is live** — worth capturing as a task (make the port injectable, or skip when the port is occupied).

**(6) A concurrent session committed to `main` mid-run, and the scoped-add rule did its job.** `41f06b9` and `e6d14c0` (agent model-routing changes) are **not this session's commits** — they landed interleaved between the task completions. Because every `git add` here enumerated exact paths and never used `git add -A`, no foreign file was ever swept into a `work` commit and no `work` file was lost to theirs. This is the ADR-0026 §5 concurrency rule working exactly as designed, observed live rather than in theory.

Two ACs were honestly left unclaimed rather than fabricated, and both need the builder: **`c6fzb` AC-7** (a dashboard-launched session's VS Code terminal tab and `/resume` picker entry showing the derived name) requires a human driving VS Code — the mechanism is verified, the manual check is not; and the **`.vsix` was not repackaged** (`vsce` is not installed), though `vscode-extension/package.json` was bumped 0.2.1 → 0.3.0. No new backlog items, no concept candidates. `todo/` is empty across all three BCs.

**Type:** Modeling / Refine
**BC:** agentic-workflow
**Status after:** todo
**Summary:** The capture's claims all re-verified against `main` (write-then-rename at task-lifecycle.mjs:240-241, no destination mkdir anywhere, the L229-231 comment contradicting the code, `doing/` absent on disk in all three BCs right now). Refinement settled the design call the capture deliberately deferred: the fix shape is **pinned to write-to-destination-then-unlink-source** (the capture's shape 2), on the tactical-modeler's invariant-driven argument — judged by residual failure, the mkdir-only patch leaves an *undetectable* status-matches-folder violation (source in `from/` carrying `to`'s status, invisible to `resolveSourceOrReject`, which probes location but never frontmatter) and corrupts the mover's own optimistic-concurrency signal (in-place rewrite bumps mtime before the fallible step, so a legitimate retry falsely rejects `stale-precondition`), while the adopted shape's only residual is a self-healing duplicate, both copies individually valid. Second ruling: a missing destination lifecycle folder is **backfilled (`mkdirSync` recursive), never rejected** — `LIFECYCLE_FOLDERS` is fixed aggregate vocabulary, disk-absence only ever means "currently empty" under git's no-empty-dirs behavior, and a structured rejection would fail-closed a legal move. The ADR question the capture left to work-time was settled at refine time: **ADR-0055 written (accepted)**, amending ADR-0054's "only disk mutation" phrasing (the mover is internally two mutations) without reopening ADR-0054's verb rulings, ADR-0007, or ADR-0038. ACs rewritten from "survive the missing folder" to "succeed against the missing folder" (verbs now create it), plus a genuine pre-unlink-failure test (file-blocks-mkdir fixture), both misleading comments (L229-231 *and* L237-239) corrected, and the residual unlink-throw window explicitly scoped out so its absence reads as decided, not overlooked. Verified no existing test asserts the old ordering (all assert end-states), so the reorder is safe. `depends_on` stays empty — prior-art siblings wq7fn/dk3vz are done, todo/ is empty everywhere.
**Split into:** none
**ADRs written:** ADR-0055 (applyTaskMove write-destination-then-unlink-source; missing destination folders backfilled, never rejected)

## 2026-07-09 18:54 -- Modeling / Refined: agentic-workflow-wq7fn - Fail-closed pre-check misses the task-counts lines — bookkeeping must compute before the move

**Type:** Modeling / Refine
**BC:** agentic-workflow
**Status after:** todo
**Summary:** The capture's premise held — `adjustIndexCount` throws after `applyTaskMove` in all three verbs, and `validateBookkeepingMarkers` covers the section markers and the protocol separator but not the `**<Label>:** N` count lines. Refinement changed the fix and widened the scope, on two builder rulings. (1) **The capture's stated Direction was rejected.** It proposed a `validateCountLines` sibling — a second pane in a hand-maintained dry-run mirror. But `removeIndexLine` / `insertIndexLineAtTop` / `adjustIndexCount` / `prependProtocolEntry` are already pure `string → string`, and `applyTaskMove:218` preserves the filename across the move, so `title`/`type`/`fileName` are readable from the source path pre-move. The verbs therefore reorder to **compute-then-write**: compute the full new INDEX + protocol content inside a `try` before the move, catch the throw as the structured rejection, move, then write. `validateBookkeepingMarkers` and its three helpers are deleted — the computation *is* the guard, so every future throw site in the bookkeeping phase is fail-closed for free. Fixes the bug class (a mirror that must chase the mutation phase's throw sites) rather than the count-line instance. (2) **A live in-system generator for the malformed state was found and folded in.** `adjustIndexCount(content,'Todo',-1)` against `**Todo:** 0` writes `**Todo:** -1` silently, because `Number('0') - 1` doesn't throw; the regex `(\*\*Todo:\*\* )(\d+)` can never match past the `-` again, so every subsequent verb in that BC throws *this very bug*, after moving files. The scenario the task defends against is reachable through normal operation, not only through an inherited bespoke index. A below-zero decrement now rejects, guarded inside `adjustIndexCount` itself — the single choke point every count mutation flows through; a separate pre-check would be the same drifting mirror this task removes. Also folded in: marker-scoping `adjustIndexCount`'s unanchored first-match replace to the `task-counts` block. **Three things settled rather than deferred.** The orchestrator's one open question — whether to use the real `applyTaskMove` as the source-missing rejection oracle (a TOCTOU where a file appearing between the read-only resolve and the mover's own resolve gets moved with no bookkeeping computed) or extract its precondition probe into a pure predicate — was pinned to the extraction, on the task's own principle that a second copy of a check drifts; it is a hard AC, not a justification AC. The `dk3vz` sibling is independent (`rotateIndexDoneList` lives in `lib/index-rotation.mjs`, unreachable from `task-lifecycle.mjs` — verified by grep): no `depends_on`/`blocks` edge, sibling cross-reference in Notes. And a refinement-time claim that the existing guard had **zero** test coverage was **wrong, and the orchestrator corrected it**: `task-lifecycle-eol.test.mjs` pins the marker rejections behaviorally for all three verbs (asserting `res.reason`, files unmoved, both files byte-identical) on a CRLF fixture — the grep for the rejection *code* missed them because they assert on `reason`. The ACs consequently pin only the real gaps (protocol `---` separator, LF fixture, `res.code`, count-line, negative-count) and carry an anti-deletion clause protecting the three existing tests, which survive the refactor because the thrown marker text still surfaces in `reason`. Title sharpened to name the fix; filename unchanged.
**Split into:** none
**ADRs written:** none yet — an AC requires the worker to author one recording the compute-then-write atomicity rule ("bookkeeping is computed before the move; the move is the last mutation before the writes"), superseding `agentic-workflow-k5n8f`'s AC #5 dry-run-mirror mechanism only, and neither ADR-0038's Rulings A/B nor ADR-0042. Next free number ~0054.

## 2026-07-09 18:41 -- Modeling / Refined: agentic-workflow-dk3vz - rotateIndexDoneList reads an unparseable done-list as empty — silent {ok:true, liveEntries:0}

**Type:** Modeling / Refine
**BC:** agentic-workflow
**Status after:** todo
**Summary:** Refined against a grounded read of `lib/index-rotation.mjs`; both captured faces confirmed on `main` and a **third found**, which then constrained the fix's shape. **Face 3:** `parseDoneListEntries` throws on missing done-list markers (deliberate, test-pinned), but `rotateAllIndexDoneLists` calls it in a bare loop with no try/catch over BCs in *sorted order* — so a marker-less `design-system` throws *after* `agentic-workflow` has already written its rewritten `INDEX.md` and a new `done-archive/YYYY-MM.md`. The manifest is lost, the exit is non-zero, and `work`'s session-end prose then says "treat a non-zero exit as a soft failure: change nothing" — stranding the completed rotation on disk, uncommitted and unmentioned. Same partial-mutation shape as sibling `agentic-workflow-wq7fn`, one layer up. **This is why the signal's shape is not cosmetic:** a top-level `ok:false` for face 1 would fire that same "change nothing" branch and strand any healthy BC's rotation from the same run. Three decisions taken with the builder. (1) **Per-BC `ok:false`, top level stays `ok:true`, exit 0** — a refusing BC returns `{ok:false, code, context, reason}` under `contexts[<bc>]` and writes nothing, while top-level `changed` still carries the healthy BCs' paths so `work` commits them. Preserves ADR-0038's "ok:false ⇒ nothing written" invariant and reuses the family's `{ok:false, code}` vocabulary rather than inventing a `warnings` channel (no precedent for one anywhere in `lib/`). (2) **Refuse when the rewrite would drop lines; report when it merely would not.** Unified rule: `ok:false` iff `(parsed===0 && nonBlank>0)` (the cap question is unanswerable) or `(unmatched>0 && rotation would fire)` (the rewrite is destructive) or markers missing; a partially-parseable *under-cap* list returns `{ok:true, rotated:false, liveEntries:N, unmatched:K}` — reported, not fatal, so the builder learns of a malformed line before it becomes a blocked rotation. The fail-closed cost was taken knowingly: a permanently malformed done-list never rotates and grows past cap, but it says so every session. The rejected alternative (carry unmatched lines through verbatim) cannot preserve *position* — an unmatched line has no `completed:` date, hence no month, hence can only stay live while the entries around it roll away; verbatim in bytes, not in order. (3) **Face 3 folded in** rather than split off: `rotateAllIndexDoneLists` catches per BC and converts the throw into `{ok:false, code:'missing-done-list-markers'}`; `parseDoneListEntries` keeps throwing and its tests stay green. Also verified: the discriminator is false-positive-safe — a genuinely empty done-list is zero non-blank lines between the markers in all three BCs, so "has non-blank lines, matched none" never fires on a fresh BC. Grew from 4 ACs to 9, including a test pinning the exit code (a non-zero exit is what re-triggers `work`'s "change nothing" branch) and a test pinning that a healthy BC sorted *before* a marker-less one still rotates and still lands in `changed`. `work`'s step-2 "silent no-op is correct" is narrowed to apply only when no BC refused and none reported unmatched lines. No orchestrator round — a bug with a settled contract; the three load-bearing decisions were made against the actual code, and a specialist pass would have re-opened closed ground.
**Split into:** none
**ADRs written:** none — the change refines a rule ADR-0047 already states rather than establishing a new one, so an AC directs the worker to add an in-place `## Amendment` to ADR-0047 (the precedent ADR-0050 set via `p8k4d` / `m3vhq`) recording the third manifest branch and the narrowed no-op.

The worker reported **3**; the verifier independently arrived at **3** (baseline 177 → 178: two licensed removals, one rewrite, three net-new). Two prompt sentences fixed a two-session systematic bias — worth folding into `references/worker-return-format.md` rather than re-typing per dispatch. (2) **The `dist/app.js` EOL phantom bit at a genuinely new site.** Previous sessions met it as commit-time noise; this time it sat in the **main tree** and aborted `git merge --squash` outright (*"Your local changes to the following files would be overwritten by merge"*), stalling integration until diagnosed. `git diff --numstat` (empty) plus a `cmp` against `git show HEAD:` (identical bytes) confirmed the phantom, and `git checkout --` cleared it. This is the **fifth** consecutive session to spend attention on this file, and the first where it blocked rather than merely muddied. A `.gitattributes` decision task is overdue and should be captured. (3) **The justification ACs did real work, both of them.** AC 6 forced a check of every `MonoId` **render** site rather than every import — the `--radius-md` trap from t896s — and the check cleared the global bump (only `kanban.js:115`/`:125` render it; `app.js:13` imports without rendering), which the verifier re-grepped independently rather than taking on trust. AC 7's `showEstimate` disposition landed on **retire**: `app/card.js` is deleted, its two pure tests removed under the AC's explicit licence, and ds-006's estimate-visibility rule recorded as superseded in the BC README. AC 8's anti-deletion clause held — the OLD-contract test was rewritten to pin the reversal, not dropped. (4) **A small piece of doc-rot is now live and worth capturing.** Four modules (`collapsible-state.js`, `drawer-state.js`, `menu-state.js`, `modal-state.js`) cite `card.js (showEstimate)` in their header comments as the exemplar of the React-free-pure-decision pattern (ADR-0014's family). That file no longer exists, so a reader chasing the reference finds nothing; `motion.js`'s `doingPulseClass` — cited alongside it in every one of the four — still stands as a live exemplar. Harmless to behavior, cheap to fix, out of scope for a card-styling task. ## 2026-07-09 17:08 -- Modeling / Captured: design-system-v08qq - TicketCard — condense to 1b: no context chip, no estimate, no timestamp, smaller type

**Type:** Modeling / Capture
**BC:** design-system
**Filed to:** todo
**Summary:** The builder wants the board card to read as 1b's "Command deck" card does — status dot, mono id, title, nothing else. Today's `TicketCard` carries a bottom meta row (folder-glyph context chip, estimate chip, `updated` timestamp) and sets its type two steps larger than 1b. Three things were settled at capture. (1) **Scope of the removal grew from the opening ask.** The builder opened with "no symbol for their bounded context" — literally, drop the folder glyph — but on being shown 1b's actual card (which has no meta row at all) took the estimate chip and the timestamp with it, leaving the row as a bare carrier for the `cornerAction` slot. That slot is load-bearing (aw-022's Refine/Promote pair) and stays; the row now renders only when a corner action exists. (2) **The flat-column objection was raised and dismissed on the builder's reasoning.** Grouping is an optional per-column lens (`board-group.js`, `FLAT_BC`), so an ungrouped column has no BC section header and dropping the chip would seem to erase the bounded context from the board. The builder's answer: the mono id *already* leads with it — ids are `<bc>-<token>` (ADR-0028), so `infrastructure-h8k2m` names its context at the top of the card. The chip was restating at the bottom what the id says at the top; it is redundant in grouped and flat columns alike. Recorded as knowingly accepted, not overlooked. (3) **Two of the three removed elements were already invisible.** `board-data.js:75-76` projects `est: '—'` and `updated: ''`, so `showEstimate('—')` is false and the timestamp is falsy — the estimate chip and timestamp render **only** in the styleguide canvas specimen, never on the real dashboard board. The sole board-visible change is the context chip. Filed straight to `todo/`: every open question was settled with the builder, leaving two named implementation decisions carried as justification ACs in the t896s mould — `MonoId`'s sizing strategy (global bump vs a `size` prop; note that `app.js` *imports* it but appears never to render it, so the `--radius-md` trap t896s hit may not apply here — re-verify at work time) and `showEstimate`'s disposition (retire the helper + its two pure tests, or keep it as an unreferenced export). Flagged for the worker: `ticket-card.test.mjs`'s "the card renders the estimate chip behind the showEstimate decision" encodes the OLD contract in its title and will go red; an AC forbids satisfying the suite by deleting it — it must be re-pinned to the reversed contract, per the aw-aqyqd precedent. Prior art surfaced and confirmed related: t896s (10px radius, same "reflect 1b" family, same component), ds-006 (introduced both the estimate-chip guard and the cornerAction slot — partially superseded here), ds-008, ds-010, xr4sb.
**ADRs written:** none — no shipped decision is superseded by a new record; ds-006's estimate-visibility rule is superseded in the BC README when the task is worked.

That reads at first glance like a pull toward non-goal 2 (*"Not a general-purpose agent harness. It is opinionated DDD or nothing"*). It is not, and the distinction is worth recording rather than assuming. Non-goal 2 forbids Agentheim **presenting its method as optional** — offering DDD as one interchangeable framework among several. Plain offers no *rival* method; it offers *no* method, for a single one-off prompt, from a launcher textarea. Its real weight is the property it forced into the model: **a mode may decline to launch.** The four legacy modes always fired because their bare commands (`/agentheim:modeling`, …) are meaningful on an empty prompt; Plain's command *is* the prompt, so an empty one has nothing to send. That is carried by a single new pure predicate, `canFirePromptMode(index, prompt)`, consulted by **both** decline paths rather than re-derived at each — `fire()`'s early return (the keyboard path: bare Enter still classifies as `launch`, so the classifier never learns about modes) and the Enter button's `disabled` prop (the click path, consuming `design-system-tfhn6`'s primitive unforked per ADR-0003). Neither AC is redundant: `disabled` blocks the click and leaves the tab order, but does nothing about a keystroke. **ADR-0050 amended in place** — no new ADR — following the `agentic-workflow-p8k4d` precedent, recording mode count 4→5, index bound `0..3`→`0..4`, the moved wrap targets, that the default/reset target (Quick Capture, index 0) is explicitly **unchanged**, and the decline-to-launch property its original text assumed away. **Three observations worth carrying forward.** (1) **Armed Plain is the one launch with no guardrail on either side.** When the board is armed, `fire()` threads `skipPermissions: true` for every mode, so an armed Plain launch runs an unconstrained prompt under `--dangerously-skip-permissions` — no skill behind it and no per-action permission prompt in front of it. The task's Notes name this as a deliberate non-change (the flag already reaches `/agentheim:work`, and it is human-clicked, so no non-goal is crossed) and it is recorded here so the choice stays visible rather than accidental. Not a defect; worth a builder's eye if the arming affordance is ever revisited. (2) The **`dist/app.js` EOL phantom vs. real-rebuild ambiguity** bit again and `git diff --numstat` again resolved it cleanly: `dashboard/dist/app.js` showed `228/226` (a genuine rebuild, required by AC 12 and committed), while `dashboard/dist/index.html` showed **zero changed lines** (pure phantom) and was reverted before the checkpoint rather than committed as noise. A `.gitattributes` decision task remains worthwhile — this is the third session to spend attention on it. (3) The **`claim` ENOENT on a git-pruned `doing/`** recurred: the mechanized `claim` threw `ENOENT` renaming into `.agentheim/contexts/agentic-workflow/doing/` because git had pruned the empty directory, leaving a partial mutation (task frontmatter rewritten, INDEX/protocol edited) that had to be reverted with `git checkout --` before `mkdir -p doing/` and a retry. ## 2026-07-09 -- Modeling / Refined: agentic-workflow-m3vhq - Prompt bar — add a "Plain" mode that runs the prompt directly on Claude

**Type:** Modeling / Refine
**BC:** agentic-workflow
**Status after:** backlog — still blocked, but on a *new* dependency this pass created. `design-system-tfhn6` (promoted to `todo/` here) must ship first; `agentic-workflow-q7r3x`, the blocker that refused promotion last pass, is now `done/`.
**Summary:** A second refinement pass run against the code `agentic-workflow-q7r3x` actually landed — and it falsified one of the first pass's acceptance criteria. AC 6 ("the Enter button renders disabled") was written when board.js still owned a board-local Enter button it could dim freely. q7r3x then replaced that with the styleguide's `EnterButton` primitive, consumed unforked (ADR-0003), whose props are exactly `{ onClick, size, ariaLabel }`. **No styleguide primitive supports a disabled state at all** — the only `disabled` anywhere in the design system is a focus-trap selector in `modal.js`. So AC 6 was unimplementable without either forking the primitive (forbidden) or faking it consumer-side with a `pointer-events: none` wrapper (an a11y lie: the `<button>` stays focusable and announces as enabled). The first pass's note "no new glyph, so no `design-system` dependency" reasoned about the *glyph* and never reached the *button*. Builder chose to close the gap on the primitive, mirroring the `design-system-xr4sb` → `agentic-workflow-q7r3x` precedent verbatim (styleguide ships it, dashboard consumes it unforked) over the wrapper or dropping the affordance. Split off **design-system-tfhn6**; m3vhq gains it as a third `depends_on` and AC 6 now names the prop. Settled tfhn6's paint as **de-emphasis by opacity** (ADR-0016), never a fill swap: `--accent-ochre` and `--accent-ochre-fg` are a contrast-matched pair (xr4sb added the latter precisely because the ochre inverts lightness across themes), so dimming both together preserves legibility in both — and the shipped guard `enter-button.test.mjs` asserts `background: "var(--accent-ochre)"` as a *literal*, so a conditional fill would break a test written to protect ADR-0048's carve-out. Also verified the rest of the task against live code: the `bot` glyph really is in `icons.js` (AC 7 holds), `PromptModeTab`'s `divider` prop is already length-derived so five cells need no change there, and `requiresPrompt` being absent on the four legacy modes makes them falsy-fire by construction (AC 3 holds). Pinned two `prompt-mode.test.mjs` assertions that silently *flip meaning* once `4` becomes a valid index and must be re-pinned rather than merely re-run (`clampPromptModeIndex(4) === 0` → `(5) === 0`; `4` leaves the `invalid` array). Recorded the standing insight that `disabled` blocks only the click path while `fire()`'s guard blocks the keyboard path — two entry points, one `canFirePromptMode` predicate, neither AC redundant.
**Split into:** design-system-tfhn6
**ADRs written:** none — tfhn6 adds a prop within ADR-0048/0051's existing carve-out and paints per ADR-0016; no shipped decision is reversed. It does reopen the styleguide gate (visible canvas change), per the standing xr4sb precedent.

## 2026-07-09 15:02 -- Modeling / Captured: agentic-workflow-h4n2v - Stop dashboard menu item calls the stop script, not the slash command

**Type:** Modeling / Capture
**BC:** agentic-workflow
**Filed to:** todo
**Summary:** The topbar settings menu's Stop dashboard item currently fires `launchOrCopy(STOP_DASHBOARD_COMMAND)`, booting a whole Claude session via the bridge just to kill a pid and delete a runfile — and in a bridge-less browser it can only copy a string, so "Stop" stops nothing. Replace it with a scoped `POST /api/stop` on the dashboard server, dispatched ahead of `server.mjs`'s 405 method gate exactly as ADR-0046's `DELETE /api/whats-next` is. Builder chose the server-self-stop seam over having the bridge shell out to `launch.mjs stop`; that reverses aw-028's "the server is never asked to stop itself" and needs an ADR naming a third write category — **runtime self-lifecycle** — sibling to lifecycle (ADR-0017, forbidden) and advisory (ADR-0027/0043/0046). The carve-out is narrower than ADR-0046's: the only file touched is `.agentheim/.dashboard/runtime.json`, which the dashboard's own launch path already wrote and no skill reads. Pinned the non-obvious constraint that `stopDashboard()` kills by pid and under `/api/stop` that pid is the process serving the request — the response must flush before the process dies or the stopped overlay never renders.

Two integration hazards worth recording: (1) the verifier's `node build.mjs` ran against the MAIN tree rather than its worktree, leaving derived `dashboard/dist/` edits that blocked the squash-merge — reverted as pure derived output (the branch already carried a verifier-confirmed-reproducible rebuild), a main-tree sibling of the known "worker rebuilds dist despite contract" hazard; (2) `git status` persistently flags `dashboard/dist/app.js` as modified when its bytes are identical to HEAD — EOL noise under `core.autocrlf=true` with no `.gitattributes`; a `git checkout --` clears it only until git next touches the index, so it is recorded as left-behind carry-over above rather than falsely reported as resolved. Worth a `.gitattributes` decision task if it keeps recurring. No bounces, no failures, no new backlog items, no concept candidates. ## 2026-07-09 -- Modeling / Refined: agentic-workflow-m3vhq - Prompt bar — add a "Plain" mode that runs the prompt directly on Claude

**Type:** Modeling / Refine
**BC:** agentic-workflow
**Status after:** backlog — auto-promotion attempted and correctly refused. The task is fully refined, but `promote` rejected it `blocked-dependency`: `agentic-workflow-q7r3x` is in `todo/`, not `done/`. Worth recording that the CLI is **stricter than the skill's prose** — `modeling`'s readiness bullet reads "dependencies known and either met **or tracked**", while `applyTaskMove` requires every `depends_on` id to sit in a `done/` folder. Re-promote once q7r3x ships; no re-refinement needed.
**Summary:** Cornered four builder decisions the capture left open: label confirmed as **Plain** (over the voice transcript's "Plane"); position is a **fifth peer appended last**, leaving Quick Capture as index 0 / the mount default / the post-launch reset target; an **empty prompt is a no-op** (Plain is the first mode that can decline to launch — the other four degrade to a meaningful bare command, Plain would fire the empty string); glyph reuses the existing `bot` key, so no `design-system` dependency. Wrote 12 concrete ACs naming the exact call sites: `plainCommandFor` (verbatim passthrough, no command prefix, no bare-command constant), a new pure `canFirePromptMode(index, prompt)` predicate consulted by both the `fire()` guard and the Enter button's disabled state, `promptBarKeyIntent` explicitly untouched (the decline lives in `fire()`, not the classifier), a 5-cycle `clampPromptModeIndex`/`nextPromptModeIndex` bound, and the `dashboard/dist/` rebuild. Added `depends_on: agentic-workflow-q7r3x` (it hard-codes "four edge-to-edge equal-width cells" and rewrites the same `BoardPromptBar`/`PromptModeTab` pair — sequencing avoids a two-worker collision in one file) plus the styleguide-gate dep; set the reciprocal `blocks` edge on q7r3x. Backlinked ADR-0050/0003/0018/0016 and the two "add a tab" precedents (h7n2c, aw-036). Recorded the armed-launch inheritance as an explicit non-change: Plain is the one mode with no skill guardrail behind `--dangerously-skip-permissions`.
**Split into:** none
**ADRs written:** none — ADR-0050 gains an `## Amendment` section at implementation time (four → five modes; a mode may decline to launch), mirroring the precedent `agentic-workflow-p8k4d` set.

## 2026-07-04 16:22 -- Modeling / Refined: agentic-workflow-n7q4d - Harden the verifier-catch-rate corpus with discriminating fixtures

**Type:** Modeling / Refine
**BC:** agentic-workflow
**Status after:** backlog
**Summary:** Cornered a conflation in the fixture-retention bar: it treated "opus shows non-zero verdict variance" as an unqualified keep signal, but opus flip-flops for two structurally different reasons — (a) hard-but-unambiguous (near its reasoning ceiling on a fixture with one right answer → a weaker tier reliably does worse; a real discriminator) vs (b) contested ground truth (even opus can't settle it → a weaker tier flip-flopping measures noise, not tier). Retaining (b) is worse than the false tie bx7k5 already guards against: it's a **false vindication of the incumbent on noise** (bx7k5 could read "opus 2/3, sonnet 1/3" as vindicating judgment-density when both tiers merely guess). Tightened the bar to require **unambiguous ground truth AND a reasoning-depth strain** — variance alone no longer suffices to retain (What + AC 3). Extended AC 4 to require a "what makes a valid tier-discriminator" methodology note in the eval README. Added two Notes bullets: the (a)/(b) distinction, and that check 8 (objective probe-shape ground truth) is the ambiguity-safe discriminator while checks 5/6/6b are where "borderline" shades into "contested." No split, no ADR; stays backlog.
**Split into:** none
**ADRs written:** none

**Summary:** Cornered a conflation in the falsification contract: ADR-0031 pins verifier→opus on TWO independent pillars — judgment-density (catch-rate, measurable) and decorrelation (gate ≠ producer tier, load-bearing, and structurally unmeasurable by a catch-rate eval). The spike measures pillar 1 only, but its old branch-1 remedy ("sonnet ties → propose verifier→sonnet") would re-correlate the worker→verifier pair — exactly what ADR-0031 rejects outright. ## 2026-07-04 14:40 -- Modeling / Refined: infrastructure-m3q7k - deriveContext can't parse a leading-digit token id

**Type:** Modeling / Refine
**BC:** infrastructure
**Status after:** backlog (promotion-ready — offered to builder)
**Summary:** Cornered the embedded decision with the `architect`. Builder chose the **Postel split** (forgiving resolver + strict mint-time lint) with the **5-char-any-lead** resolver style — loosen only deriveContext's token branch to `[0-9a-hjkmnp-tv-z]{5}` (still rejects `uuuuu` / 6-char). Surfaced two findings that reshaped the task: (1) the current "return id unchanged on a leading-digit token" is a **deliberate, tested** aw-078 choice (test at task-lifecycle.test.mjs:352) — hardening reverses it, so the AC now calls to rewrite that test + the doc comment, not patch around them; (2) the old AC #3 was **factually wrong** — `duplicate-id-check.mjs` is charter-bound shape-agnostic (no tail parse), so `deriveContext` is the sole dual-shape regex and there is no sibling to keep in sync; AC #3 dropped. Fix #2 lands as a new pure `lib/id-grammar.mjs` (`classifyTaskId`/`isWellFormedTaskId`/`findMalformedTaskIds` + `node --test` live-tree scan, aw-080 shape) with a `GRANDFATHERED_IDS` allowlist for the un-renumberable `5w5gs`; reserved all-digit foundation ids need no entry. Rewrote Why/What/AC/Notes accordingly.
**Split into:** none
**ADRs written:** none yet — AC calls for a short ADR amending ADR-0028 §3–§4 as the first work commit (resolver now digit-lead-tolerant; minters stricter than the parser; grammar enforced by the lint).

## 2026-07-03 14:45 -- Modeling / Captured: infrastructure-5w5gs - task-lifecycle CLI breaks on CRLF .agentheim files

**Type:** Modeling / Capture
**BC:** infrastructure
**Filed to:** backlog
**Summary:** `lib/task-lifecycle.mjs` assumes LF line endings in its INDEX/protocol edit functions (`removeIndexLine` start-marker regex `-->\n`, `insertIndexLineAtTop` `indexOf('-->\n')`, `prependProtocolEntry` `indexOf('\n---\n\n')`), so on CRLF `.agentheim` files — the Windows norm — a `promote` throws *after* `applyTaskMove` has already moved the file, stranding the board in a half-promoted state. Discovered live promoting `design-system-pv3mq` in the Mediatheca consumer repo (that promote was reconciled by hand). Captured as a bug with a concrete fix map (normalize EOL+BOM on the read/write boundary, keep the regex logic unchanged) and acceptance criteria covering CRLF/LF/BOM round-trips + new `lib/test/` fixtures. Left under-refined in backlog; optional hardening noted (dry-validate markers before moving so it fails closed).

Detected immediately, `git reset --mixed` to before the batch, and rebuilt all three with explicit scoped `git add` so each task's code landed in its own commit. Lesson for future runs: when a worker pre-stages via `git mv`, either `git commit -- <paths>` or reset-and-rebuild — a bare `git commit` is not scoped by the preceding `git add`.
